# 3D render: how to measure, and what every experiment concluded

**Read this before proposing or starting any render change.** It is the index and the standing rules;
it is meant to stay small. The full write-up of every experiment — landed, reverted or rejected, with
its numbers and its traps — lives in [`3d-changes.md`](3d-changes.md). Find an entry there by grepping
the heading named in the verdict table below.

Building a thing out of quads rather than generating it: [`3d-geometry.md`](3d-geometry.md) is the
how-to — the per-face texture split, the 0..1 aspect lock, values, wiring and warm. HAND-MODELLING it
instead, when the brief is a number: [`3d-models.md`](3d-models.md) § Hand-modelling in Blender over
MCP, which is a live `blender` MCP against the running Blender and not an offline exporter.

**Appending after you try something:** write the entry in `3d-changes.md`, add **one row** here. The
row is the index; an entry with no row is invisible. Keep entries to ~25 lines — what changed, the
measured numbers, the trap, the verdict. Record settled outcomes only: no `IN PROGRESS`, no `pending`,
no "checked X and it was clean". If it is not settled, it is not an entry yet. When a new entry
invalidates an old one, mark the old heading `> SUPERSEDED by <heading>`. When `3d-changes.md` reaches
~2600 lines, `git mv` it to `3d-changes-<YYYY-MM>.md` and start a fresh one.

## Baselines

All numbers come from one of two machines, and **most of the guidance inverts between them**:

- **RTX 3050**, 60fps cap, 1080p-ish, `vidAntialias=4`. `submit`-bound: the CPU draw-call wall is the
  ceiling and a better GPU does not move it.
- **Integrated Vega** (`AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics 0x1638`), added 2026-07-28. **Fill-bound**: `GPU=`
  exceeds `submit=`, `calls` do not move with window size, and draw-call work is not the lever. See
  "the frame is GPU-bound on an integrated Radeon" in the log before applying anything here to it.

**Which view a number came from matters more than the number.** Gameplay is a zoom lerp, not a set of
modes (`RenderConfig.CAMERA`, `CameraRig`): zoom 0 = close, ~30° above ground; zoom 1 = top-down.
Resting targets are capped (`parasiteZoom: 0.30`, `hostZoom: 0.60`) and `tactical` is pinned at 1.0.
Measured across views and spots, **gameplay runs 158–288 calls**. Separately, the street-debug
**free-cam** (backtick) can go fully parallel and render the whole city — ~230 buildings — which no
gameplay camera does. It is a dev tool; the two-tier merge lead below is the only entry targeting it.

## How to measure (and how not to)

- **`calls=` in the `[street-render]` trace is the only draw-call number.** Enable the `perf street`
  console toggle (`render.Actors.DEBUG_PERF`) or street-debug mode (backtick). The trace is emitted
  by `StreetPerf.hx:228` (`frame=/GPU=/submit=/upd=/idle=/calls=/tris=/programs=/shdw=/heap=`).
- **The scene dump (`9`) is an INVENTORY, not a draw list.** `visibleDrawables` counts objects whose
  `visible` chain is true — frustum culling then discards most of them. Real ratio measured here:
  **1616 visibleDrawables → 435 calls**. A big number in the dump means nothing until you confirm
  those objects survive culling. Two failed experiments came from reading the dump as draws.
- **`submit` is the CPU draw-call wall** — time inside `composer.render()` (scene walk + issuing
  draws + any driver blocking). `upd` (engine CPU) is ~1ms and irrelevant. `GPU=` is a real timer
  query (`EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2`). Compare the two before assuming which wall you are on:
  they sit at `4.75` vs `4.66` with AO off, but AO flips the frame GPU-bound. "Submit is the wall" is
  the default, not a law — and on the iGPU it is simply false.
- **Always A/B against a measured baseline in the same view.** Both `follow` and `tactical` — they
  differ (tactical draws ~80 more calls and ~2ms more submit).
- **A surface's value is `built_linear` in `art/textures.json`** — stamped for every entry by
  `make tex`, so the ladder is a grep and shows up in a diff. It is mean linear LUMINANCE (Rec.709,
  per pixel, over the OPAQUE texels), which is the statistic every row below is written in and is
  *not* the flat channel mean: on the green-dominant wilderness set the two differ by 3-9%, enough to
  invert which of two layers reads lighter. For what the manifest does not hold — a region of a
  captured frame, an atlas cell, a prop's hue split — `python3 tools/pix.py <img> [--box
  name,x0,y0,x1,y1] [--scene] [--atlas r,c] [--hue]`, same statistic. Reproduces the recorded
  asphalt 0.0329, walkway 0.0680, turf 0.0476, earth 0.0348 and roof 0.0275.

### Measuring on a weak / integrated GPU

The numbers lie unless you do all of this — every rule here was paid for by a wrong conclusion:

- **Pin the camera.** `render.Tools.freeCam` is a static, so
  `parasiteHx['render.Tools'].freeCam.lookFrom(...)` reproduces an exact frame across reloads *and*
  rebuilds (verified: 209/210/211 calls, 104,480/104,482/104,484 tris across three builds).
- **Interleave A/B/A/B and take the MEDIAN.** Means get destroyed by outliers — one run turned a real
  −19% win into a bogus −7% loss.
- **Warm the shader permutations first.** Keys `5`/`7` change the program key; the first toggle
  compiles a fresh set inside your measurement window. `(COMPILE)`-tagged trace lines are filtered.
- **Never compare across sessions.** An integrated Radeon switches power state mid-run: the identical
  pinned frame was measured at both 11.61ms and 4.06ms. Shares (`1 − off/on`) survive clock drift;
  absolute ms do not.
- **The game window must be FOCUSED and in the foreground.** Backgrounded, the compositor throttles to
  ~1fps and the timer query spans the whole idle gap, so `GPU` reads ~**1015ms** and an A/B comes out
  *inverted*. Any scripted sweep should filter samples (`GPU < 100`) and check the topbar fps.
- **Verify the thing the system is actually keyed on.** The lamp pool keys on distance to the PLAYER,
  so a free-cam-only screenshot A/B cannot see what lowering it costs — and nearly shipped a bad default.

### Attributing every draw call (the census)

`calls=` gives a total but not a culprit. To get the real per-object breakdown **without touching
shipped code**, patch from CDP (`three.global.js` exposes `window.THREE`):

- `Object3D.prototype.onBeforeRender` fires once per **post-cull** draw (`WebGLRenderer.js:2014`) —
  the only honest "what actually drew" list. Its first arg **is the renderer**, which is the one way
  to reach the live renderer from JS (`parasiteHx` is statics-only).
- Wrap `renderer.renderBufferDirect` (an **instance** property, `WebGLRenderer.js:1104`, not on the
  prototype) and read `renderer.info.render.calls` before/after each call — the delta attributes
  draws exactly, instead of assuming one call per object.
- Classify by the `scene` arg: `null` → **shadow pass** (`WebGLShadowMap` calls `renderBufferDirect`
  directly and never fires `onBeforeRender`, so shadow draws are invisible to the object hook);
  `scene.isScene === true` → main scene; anything else → **post fullscreen quad**.
- In the shadow pass `material` is the depth override — read `object.material.userData.cls` for
  identity. In the main pass `onBeforeRender` receives the **group's** material, so multi-material
  walls attribute per face (better than the dump).
- Wrap the body in `try/catch`: an exception inside the wrapper propagates into the render loop and
  silently yields an empty census.

Measured this way, **tactical = 513 calls: 175 shadow / 324 main / 14 post**.

## Verdict table

Bodies in [`3d-changes.md`](3d-changes.md) and its dated predecessors (`3d-changes-2026-10.md`
back) — grep the change text.

### Perf and render engineering

| change | result | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `flattenBox` + `mergeBand` | 10,595 → ~3,000 calls free-cam, 6 → 30fps | **landed** `6a20c75` `8240e4d` |
| MSAA via composer render targets | real AA, was previously absent entirely | **landed** `729c064` |
| Parapet ring merge per building | 583 → 436 calls, submit 10 → 7.3ms | **landed** `1183cff` |
| Ghost overlay capacity fix | fixes a hard `RangeError` crash | **landed** `5a6ae04` |
| `forceSinglePass` on decal materials | 513 → 458 calls (−11%), no visual change | **landed** |
| Merged city-wide shadow caster | shadow pass 175 → 16 calls | **landed** |
| ^ the two together, measured in-game | calls −44/−52%, **submit −25%**, +2ms idle | **landed** |
| Decals sample the atlas (per-instance UV rect) | 55 → **2** decal calls; 210 → 158 total | **landed** |
| `allowOverride:false` + `OVERLAY_EPS` | fixes 2 invisible-until-AO-on z-fight bugs | **landed** |
| GTAO ambient occlusion | +140 calls, +5.4ms GPU; flips the frame GPU-bound | **landed, default OFF** `870d0f8` |
| Roof-detail material hoist | ~750 → 6 materials; **0 draw calls** | **landed** (hygiene only) |
| Corpse-vs-blood: `depthWrite` + per-cell Y slots | killed the flip, bought per-pixel z-fight flicker | **reverted — worse** |
| Corpse-vs-blood: `renderOrder` tiers | stable layering, extra draw only in corpse cells | **landed** |
| Wall graffiti vs doors: door-span overlap skip | decals no longer clip doors | **landed** |
| Gas-burst shader pre-warm (4 programs) | first burst 167ms spike → **0 new programs** | **landed** |
| Boot pre-warm at the menu | first city entry ~2s black → **7** programs | **landed** |
| Actors + ground decals `receiveShadow` | `receiveShadow` is a UNIFORM: no recompile, no call delta | **landed** |
| Window light switches (scale-0 stand-in) | calls 151 → 151, +37 meshes, lit ratio stationary | **landed** |
| Object marks: tactical ring + through-wall x-ray | **+1 call, +2 tris** per marked object surviving cull | **landed** |
| iGPU is FILL-bound, not submit-bound | `GPU 11.2 > submit`; calls flat as the window grows | **diagnosis — inverted the ranking** |
| GPU-side A/B keys `V` / `Shift+1` / `Shift+5` | isolates render scale, bloom alone, cones alone | **landed** (permanent) |
| Render scale (`vidRenderScale`, default 100) | 1.25 → 1.00 = **−29% GPU**, zero visual change | **landed** |
| `LAMP_LIGHT.pool` 12 → 6 as a new default | −19% GPU but spends a tuned art value | **reverted** |
| ^ shipped as `vidLampLights` (Off/4/8/12, def. 12) | linear **0.32ms per light**; Off = −42% | **landed** |
| **MeshStandard → MeshLambert on city surfaces** | **−42% GPU**, calls/tris unchanged | **landed — largest win here** |
| `vidBloom` (Off/On, default On) | −3.1…3.4ms (22–24%), calls 151 → 138 | **landed** |
| AO pass was sized in CSS px | wrong AO resolution once render scale shipped | **fixed** |
| Re-attribution after Lambert | floor 32% / spots 31% / bloom 25% / shadows 15% / cones 0% | **current ranking** |
| Shadow box size (`MOON_SHADOW.halfExtent`) | best safe win ~21 calls; coverage-bound below 75 | **not a lever** |
| BatchedMesh for roof details | **+4 calls, +2ms submit, −3ms idle** | **reverted — worse** |
| Atlas the WALL textures like the decals | walls tile; an atlas cannot wrap inside a sub-rect | **rejected — wrong tool** |
| Texture array for walls (3 calls/box → 1) | ~20 calls in one sample; enables the static merge | **open lead for the city; DONE for corp (`CorpMerge`)** |
| Two-tier static city merge | ~3k → ~200 calls **in the debug free-cam only** | **open lead, deferred** |
| Bloom as Off / Low / High | needs the pass rebuilt; raising the threshold saves nothing | **rejected** |
| Pull the fog / view distance in | shorter sightlines — look/feel | **rejected** |
| Per-building merges of doors / covers / roof furniture | low ROI; doors are ~5 calls in view | **rejected** |
| AgX / Neutral tone mapping | look rejected | **rejected** |
| `reversedDepthBuffer` | flips custom `depthFunc`/decal offsets; doors vanish | **rejected** |
| Distance-cull far detail | parapets are the roofline silhouette — pops | **rejected** |
| SSRPass (reflections) | style rules ban glossy/specular reflections | **rejected** |
| `RenderPixelatedPass` | total aesthetic pivot, not an enhancement | **rejected** |
| `forceSinglePass` on `LightCone` as a perf item | cones measure 0% of the frame, twice | **rejected as perf** (valid hygiene) |
| Facility frame is **97.8% prop triangles** (28,424 of 1.31M is the built world) | props 3.74ms fixed + 2.6/MP, world 0.46 + 4.39/MP — props are TRIANGLE-bound, world is fill-bound | **diagnosis — inverted the ranking for prop areas** |
| Sub-pixel triangles: shrub is 4955 tris over a **42px** silhouette = 0.56px/tri | a triangle under a pixel still costs a full 2x2 quad, so the shader runs per-TRIANGLE | **standing note** |
| **glb props → `MeshLambertMaterial` (`Models.toLambert`)** | shipped build @1920x1017: gameplay **18.5 → 12.5ms**, park in frustum **29.2 → 17.5ms (30 → 47fps)** | **landed — largest facility win** |
| Park shrub stops casting (`GREEN.casts`) | −292k tris (22% of the frame) for −0.29ms — shadow tris are 0.08ms/100k | **landed** (tri count is not the cost) |

| ^ the three-case rule, and why the inside case is NOT "extend the top edge" | `diag` floor → pull IN by `c` (**no extra triangle**, the quad becomes a trapezoid; the two wedges already share their bottom corner, so closing the top closes the seam). `diag` solid + `perp` floor → straight run, nothing. `diag` solid + `perp` solid → a vertical return triangle, i.e. a real chamfer STOP. Extending instead slides the wedge under the diagonal cell's un-inset cap and opens a void — that cap would then need an L notch, 4 tris instead of 2 on the biggest surface down there | **landed — +1 tri per inside end, 0 per outside end** |

| ^ what it costs: nothing may assume `WALL_H` | decals clamped to `WALL_H - CAP_CHAMFER` → `faceH`, the LOWER of the face's two corners. Ledge clutter sat at `WALL_H + LEDGE_DECAL_Y` → `capAt` per decal corner, evaluated on the same two triangles `cap()` emits | **3 consumers, all real; the floor pass is genuinely flat and keeps its constant** |
| ^ verified headless | **every cap vertex on `capY` of its own lattice point, 0 off**, none above `WALL_H`, none below `WALL_H − CAP_SAG` (range 2.601–3.000). Wall tris **352 = 84×4 + 16 exactly**, so the sag added no geometry, and the mitre seam test still shows **0 interior misses**. Ledge decals 284 verts all at exactly `LEDGE_DECAL_Y` over the surface, max tilt 0.182 within one decal. Vertical strips 12, tops 2.370–2.664 (were all 2.750), **0 over their own wall** | **landed** |
| **The sag shipped a hairline down every inside corner** | a 1px pure-black line out of every concave corner, texture and shading continuous across it — both sides are cap. `cap()` sampled `capY` at corners the chamfer had pulled back, and `capY` rounds an inset corner to the lattice point it came from. The un-inset neighbour draws the same two lattice heights over the FULL cell, the inset one over 3.75 → `gap = (CAP_CHAMFER/CELL)·Δh`, max **0.025** | **fixed — off-lattice vertices take `capAt`, not `capY`** |

| ^ the trap: it is TWO samplers, not one | the wall FACE top must stay on the lattice (`capY - k`) — give it `capAt` and the two perpendicular faces of an inside corner sample at their own inset offsets and the crack moves into the wall. Chamfer stop likewise: apex on the lattice (`capY`), other top vertex on the inset plane (`capAt`). Face and wedge stop being exactly `k` apart, by ≤0.025 | **landed** |

| ^ the fix is to strike from the **body's own SURFACE** | origin on the surface (`r` narrowed by the prop's own taper — the profile the surface motes' helix already uses), direction straight out from there, so the bolt cannot re-enter the body whatever the swing. | ^ **a plane-confined bolt can only go left, right and up** | so the path moved to world 3D: a strike azimuth about the prop's axis (`az`, a half-range centred on world +Z — forward and both flanks, not the hidden back) swung by `spread`. | ^ ^ and that cross product has a **real degeneracy** | a bolt aimed exactly along the view has no width axis, and `normalize(vec3(0))` is NaN — one NaN texel blacks out the whole frame through the bloom downsample, per the entry above | **guarded with an arbitrary fallback axis; such a bolt is a point on screen anyway** |
| ^ the kink bells to 0 at the **ROOT only** (`sqrt(f)`, not `sin(PI·f)`) | belling both ends pulls the tip back onto the centreline and the bolt reads as a smooth worm instead of something that snapped | **landed** |

| ^ measured spikiness, not asserted | per-frame change over 5 frames at 0.45s: **mean 14.12 on one interval vs 1.79 / 2.93 / 3.86** on the others. A drift would be flat across all four | **`gazeRate 0.07` (~2.1s hold, 1.6-3.0s across the eyes), `gazeSnap 0.06` (~0.13s flick)** |

| ^ the outward anchor must sit BEHIND the eye field, not among it | all 8 eyes are on one near-flat face at z 0.05-0.12, so their own centroid lands at z 0.10 — in front of the central eye, whose outward vector would then be 0.02 long and point anywhere | **authored `az: -0.35`; Gram-Schmidt, not a `cross(up,fwd)` basis, which blows up on the eyes facing near-straight-up** |

| ^ tendril sweep `rate` 0.25 → **0.125** | the tips travel the same distance either way, so speed only decides whether it reads as drifting or as flicking | **~8s sweep** |

A wall that would occlude the trail is faded by `Occlusion`, and below `ghostCross` the real mesh is hidden while only the `depthWrite:false` ghost draws, so it still reads through exactly like the actor sprites, the blood decals and its own landing puddles** |

### FX, UI and the view itself

| change | result | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Slime trail (crawl ribbon + landing puddle) | hand-built strip, **1 draw call**, ~26 verts | **landed** |
| Mouse move-path preview + click-to-move in 3D | **2 draw calls** while a walkable tile is hovered | **landed** |
| Rename `StreetView` → `render.View`, `#streetview` → `#view` | pure rename, no render change | **landed** |
| `vidBrightness` (50–150%, default 100) | exposure is a per-frame uniform: no recompile, no re-assert | **landed** |
| Lobbed projectiles (`arc`) + a `'blood'` projectile type | 3 extra sprites per swing on the existing pooled batch | **landed** |
| **Facility Phase 3b: the compound gets light SOURCES — masts, ceiling fixtures, window spill** | 3a made the interior its own lighting ENVIRONMENT and gave it no sources: rooms lifted uniformly (which never says WHERE the light is), the lot lit by nothing but the moon, and a lit building giving away nothing from across it. Placement is off the tile grid, deterministic, no rng: a three-row table of (surface stood on, surface bordered) — walkway/road, lot/walkway, grass/walkway — gated on `(col + row) % 6`, plus one fixture per `m.rooms` entry | **35 masts, 25 fixtures over 25 rooms, 12 pool slots claimed. **0 new programs** — `FacilityScene` has built the full 12-slot pool with no bulbs since phase 0 for `NUM_SPOT_LIGHTS` parity, so the lights were already in every program key and already running their loop. `LampShadows` and the 512² caster maps arrive free off `active()`. Nothing flickers, so `LightCone.pulse` is never called and the per-frame cost is one `update()`** |
| ^ **an additive quad authored against a 0..1 intuition ANNIHILATES this art** | `C_POOL` derived from `LAMP_COLOR`'s `Y_linear` 0.435 gave 0.25, which measured **Y 0.530 against a lit lab floor's 0.0168 — 31x the surface it lies on**. The error is in the SURFACES, not the blend: floor albedo is 0.0607 linear and interiors render around **Y 0.017**, so nothing here is predictable from the additive colour | **fit off the built frame instead: ~**1.3 per unit C**, not the 2.05 one blown-out sample suggested — so the first correction (0.006, 1.47x) undershot as badly as 0.25 overshot. Landed `C_POOL` **0.012 = 1.93x** the same room's plain floor. Spill 0.02 puts the pavement under an opening at 0.020-0.038 against 0.006-0.009 in the gaps, an 18-sample scan** |
| ^ ONE merged additive mesh for four kinds of quad | lamp heads, ceiling panels, room floor pools and window/door spill differ only in BRIGHTNESS | **which rides `MeshBufTools.quadC`'s per-vertex grey, so one material carries hue + HDR multiplier and one draw call carries the lot. `vertexColors` IS a program key — warmed in `facScene`. The ceiling panel is its own mesh, NOT in `Shell.roof`, so it survives the fade; from outside the opaque roof depth-rejects it, because additive with `depthWrite` off still depth-TESTS** |
| ^ the prop's head is NOT at its placement point | `Models.normalize` centres the bbox in X/Z, and this glb's mast sits at local **z −0.062** with the housing hanging off toward +z — a bulb at the placement point hangs in mid air beside the fixture | **`LAMP_HEAD_Y/Z` measured off the built glb, and `LAMP_H` 5.9 is chosen so `LAMP_H * LAMP_HEAD_Y` = **5.60** = the city street bulb's own height, landing `LampShadows`' `refY / post.y` on exactly 1.0. `DoorCell.inDir` is the side the LEAVES SWING into, not the side the lot is on — the spill finds the outdoor side itself, on the perpendicular axis only** |
| ^ **the facility warm scene never renders, so its `depth` programs were never warmed** | a facility entry adds 5 programs, all `depth` — and adds the SAME 5 with the whole lighting pass switched off behind a temporary flag (masts 0, ceilings 0) | **so 3b costs nothing, and the 5 are a STANDING GAP: `compileAsync` does not compile shadow-map depth programs, and only the CITY warm scene is ever rendered (`comp.render()` at the end of warmup). A city entry shows the same shape at 12 (5 depth + 5 lambert + 2 physical). Not fixed here** |
| ^ **the head quad was the wrong tool, and a TRELLIS atlas does NOT have to be hand-painted** | a FRONTAL quad is fixed-yaw by construction; a mast YAWS to face the surface it lights, so the card sat across the housing at an arbitrary angle and read as a sticker. `3d-models.md`'s "hand-painting an emissive map into an unknown atlas layout is not worth it" is true and does not apply — it can be GENERATED | **rasterize the UV triangles whose centroid is above y 0.44 and past z 0.035 in the model's own frame (the head box, not the arm) into a 512 mask, dilate 2px into the chart gutter, tint with `LAMP_COLOR` because `models.mjs` pins `emissiveFactor` to `[1,1,1]`, 0 new programs. Rasterize off the BUILT glb, never the 100k master, or the mask misses a chart the decimation moved** |
| ^ **the whole head box was too much, and the FIX for it was the wrong theory** | the head is a flat SLAB, so its biggest face is the top, pointing straight at a camera that looks down: it read as a glowing white card. Filtering by averaged `NORMAL` (`ny < -0.5`) was then reported as still wrong, with white polys on the pole, blamed on a generated mesh's normals | **the normals were fine. Measured instead of believed: rasterize the mask, test every UNSELECTED triangle's UV centroid against it — **22 sampled a lit texel, y −0.498..+0.484, z −0.057**, i.e. the mast down to the base plate. It was the **2px DILATION**, which on a mosaic atlas reaches the next chart, and that chart is an unrelated part of the surface** |
| ^ **[`tools/emissive.py`](../tools/emissive.py): a box, a bleed mask, and a preview you can look at** | selection is a bounding box on the triangle centroid, no normals; the dilation is masked against the coverage of every unselected triangle, so it may enter the gutter and never another chart | **the bleed count is the gate. Author at the entry's own `tex` (320), never 512: `models.mjs` resizes it and the resize re-introduces the ramp the mask just removed. The 3-view preview is framed ON THE SELECTION, since a lens is 2% of a 6-metre mast** |
| ^ **and that mask STILL bled, because both halves rasterized by pixel CENTRE** | `PIL.ImageDraw.polygon` fills by centre, so **a chart thinner than a texel covers nothing at all** — and a TRELLIS atlas is full of those. Invisible to the bleed mask, still sampled by the GPU. The bleed CHECK had the matching hole: it tested each unselected triangle's UV CENTROID, and a triangle with one lit corner still draws a speck. Re-audited, the "clean" mask bled onto **9 triangles, y −0.476..+0.463, z −0.074** — the pole | **rasterize CONSERVATIVELY: 4x supersample max-pooled down (a texel is covered if ANY part of a triangle falls in it) + `outline=255` for a triangle even that misses, and grow the unselected coverage 1 texel before subtracting, because bilinear reads a 2x2 neighbourhood. Check per-TRIANGLE footprint, bbox-rejected first. 9 → 0** |
| ^ **where an emissive goes is an ART call the camera makes, not a physics one** | the head is 0.35 x 0.20 x 0.43 world units and its lens faces DOWN, so the physically-correct lens-only map is invisible from an overhead camera — while lighting the whole slab put its biggest face, the top, at the camera and read as a white card | **shipped `y ≤ 0.4845`: lens + sides, **top excluded**. 194 tris, 7.0% of the surface — a bright edge on a dark housing from the game camera, a glowing head from anywhere lower** |
| ^ **`LAMP_CONE.startFrac` is NEGATIVE, and that is a street-lamp fact** | −0.1 lifts the shaft a tenth of the bulb height ABOVE the bulb, right for a lamp whose glowing hood sits over its light position. A facility mast's `bulbY` 5.605 already IS the lens plane, so the cone started at **6.17** — a third of a unit clear of the top of the whole 5.9 post, hanging in the air over the lamp | **optional `startFrac` on `LightConeOpts`, 0 for the facility. Material untouched, so no program key moves** |
| ^ **the lit panes were bright and never GLOWED, by exactly one factor** | bloom runs on the composer's LINEAR target, before `OutputPass` tone-maps, so a pane blooms when `texel_linear * emissiveIntensity` clears `BLOOM_THRESHOLD` 0.9. The art's glass band is sRGB 144-207 = linear **0.26-0.62** — the same histogram `addPane` splits its alpha on — so `WINDOW_EMISSIVE` 0.55 put the brightest glass texel at **0.34** and nothing crossed | **2.2 lands the band at 0.57-1.36: the bright half of every pane blooms onto the facade around it and the dark frame (16-127) never does. One constant, no geometry — the halo IS the "occupied building" read the ground spill alone could not give** |
| ^ the ceiling pair parked behind `CEIL_GLOW = false` | the panel and the floor pool, off for now on request | **an inline `false`, so the block folds out. The fixtures themselves still exist and still claim spotlight slots — only the two drawn quads are gone, and the merged glow mesh survives on window/door spill alone at the same draw call** |
| ^ **a rectangle of light reads as a projected slide, not as an aperture** | the spill patch was a rect, and light out of a finite opening fans — every point of the aperture lights every point of the ground | **`SPILL_SPREAD` 4.0 per side over `SPILL_DEPTH` 8.0 = a ~27 degree fan. The uv stays 0..1 so the shared gradient still fits the trapezoid: two numbers, no extra geometry, no extra draw. Wide enough to read, narrow enough that neighbouring runs do not merge into one band** |
| ^ ^ **and the patch STILL read round, because the alpha map is a squircle** | `makeRectGlowGradient`'s corner radius is 0.16 of the canvas against a half-extent of 0.30 — **0.14 of straight run**, so the shape is nearly a circle. Right for the sewer wall fixture's diffuser it was written for, wrong for ground cut by the straight jambs of an opening; worst on a DOOR, whose patch is one cell wide and read as a blob | **optional `radFrac`, default 0.16 (sewer byte for byte), 0.0 for the facility. Measured on the generated canvases: corner texel (14,14) **233 → 255**, diagonal (8,8) **0 → 92**, the straight edges and the row profile identical. The falloff still curves round the corner, which is what a soft edge does** |
| ^ **a window casts NO fake actor shadow, because every caster came from `LampLights.active()`** | `View` hands that one array to `Actors.setLamps`, so a light with no pool slot is invisible to `LampShadows` — and a window must not take a slot, since 12 are already shared by the masts and the ceiling fixtures | **new `Area3D.shadowLamps()`: a static built-once `LampPost` list that reaches `LampShadows` and nothing else, `[]` in the city/tunnels/wilderness where every light does own a slot. The HEIGHT then does the art for free — `LampShadows` scales length and reach by `refY / post.y`, and `SPILL_Y` = `WALL_H / 2` = 3.0 against 5.6 gives `low` **1.87**, reach **12** units = 3 cells, `lenMul` **1.12**: long shadows over a tight pool, which is what a window does. One source per CELL of a run, not per run — reach is 3 cells and a 3-cell run is 3 cells long, so a centred source would shadow ALONG the wall** |
| **The tactical grid could not express a blocked cell — 80% of them drew as open ground** | the marks sit on CORNERS, and a corner is marked when ANY of its 4 cells is walkable, so an obstacle's own corners are marked by its NEIGHBOURS. Measured in a forest window: 83 blocked cells, **66 cost the lattice zero marks**, all 83 together cost 11 of 1054. Only a 2x2+ blob drops an interior corner, and one missing cross reads as nothing | **second pass in the same `build()`: dashed perimeter (edges facing a WALKABLE neighbour only) + diagonal hatch. Borrows `OCCLUSION.outlineDash`/`Gap` 0.6+0.4 = exactly 4 dashes per cell edge, so it IS the rectangle `Occlusion` draws round a faded footprint. **0 extra draw calls** — same mesh, same material. 2306 cross + 1268 dash + 609 hatch = 4183 quads, predicted and measured to the quad** |
| ^ the hatch is a WORLD-space line family clipped per cell, and `HATCH` 2.0 was wrong | `x + z = k * HATCH`, so a stripe continues into the next blocked cell instead of stopping at the shared edge and a thicket hatches as ONE shape. `'diag'` was the last unclaimed fill in the game's pattern vocabulary (XRAY took `scan`, OBJMARK took `dots`) | **at 2.0 (3 stripes/cell) three long strokes read as random SCRATCHES, and the corner-to-corner stripe at 5.66 units beat the 0.6-unit dashes it sits inside. 1.0 (7 stripes) reads as fill. Density, not width, is what turns strokes into a pattern** |
| ^ city buildings excluded, and that is correctness not thrift | their footprints are unpaved (`Ground` paves road/alley/walkway only) so the dashes hang in midair under a wall that hides them — and once the block fades, which in tactical is the whole selected block, `Occlusion` draws that identical rectangle a hair above | **two coincident HDR dash runs through bloom. Skipping `TILE_BUILDING` leaves what actually surprises a player: barrels, unwalkable objects, blocking decoration** |
| ^ **that exclusion was untested and skipped every FACILITY wall too** | reported as the walls having no hashing or dotting, "only windows". A facility wall IS `TILE_BUILDING` (`TEMP_BUILDING_WALL`/`INNER_WALL`/`VENT` all map to tile 2) and a facility WINDOW is not (`TILE_WINDOW1`/`WINDOWH1..V3`, 35-41) — both unwalkable, only one hit the guard, so the windows marked and the wall around them did not. `CorpAreaGenerator` uses the same three tiles | **`area.isCity() &&` in front of the tile test — city behaviour byte for byte. Every reason the guard gives is absent in a facility: the wall is a half-cell slab with real floor either side (`FacilityGeom.strips`), `floorY` is 0, no `Occlusion` runs there. Measured in one window: **330** blocked, **186 skipped**, 144 drawn (38 of them windows)** |
| ^ **the per-endpoint height sample read the WRONG CELL, and a bench hatched half** | a clipped stripe's endpoints lie exactly ON a cell boundary and `CityConfig.worldToCell` is `Math.round`: `x0`/`z0` round back into this cell, `x1`/`z1` into the NEXT one. Invisible until a surface where two edge-sharing cells stand 2.0 apart — `FacilityTables.groundY` is `BENCH_H` over a table cell and 0 beside it — so a stripe on the block's `+x`/`+z` rim drew at `y=0.06`, inside the casework. Measured on one 3x2 island, endpoints on the worktop: 14/14 interior, 10/14 one outer side, **6/14** far corner | **`hatchY` nudges the SAMPLE `EPS` 0.01 toward the cell centre; the vertex still lands on the true endpoint, so the geometry is unchanged and the wilderness slope answers the same. After: 14/14 everywhere. Not a triangle per cell — the block's rim, which over a run reads as half a table** |
| ^ `addEdge` → `addSeg`, and a new `invalidate()` | the hatch is 45 degrees and needed arbitrary heading; and a flat quad at the cell-CENTRE sample floats/sinks over wilderness relief | **per-endpoint heights, normal-widened, one emitter for crosses/dashes/hatch. `View.refreshObjects` now invalidates: the grid rebuilds only when the player's CELL moves, and an organ grown under a standing player moves walkability without moving the player — invisible before this pass, a stale outline after it** |
| **Facility Phase 4: the ground steps, the kerb arrives, the lot gets paint and the park gets a layout** | outdoor dressing plus two asks — pavement borders "like on the streets" at both the road and the park, and the road and the park ground "a little below the walkway". THE SIGN IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE CITY'S and that is the design: `Ground` raises its walkway and leaves the road at 0, but `generateBuilding` rings EVERY structure with a 2-cell pavement apron and an indoor floor is y = 0, so raising the pavement steps down into every doorway and floats every wall base | **the pavement and the LOT stay at 0 with the floors, walls, doors, benches and glow quads; only the two surfaces nothing stands on drop. `KERB_DROP` = `RenderConfig.CURB_H` = 0.2. The lot stays up for one more reason: the sidewalk's 4x2 parking entry would be a kerb across a driveway. Measured over the built grid — road and every park tile **−0.20**, walkway/lot/wall/floor **0.00**, bench cells **2.00**. **0 console errors, 109 programs**, the same +5 depth-only gap 3b recorded** |
| ^ **one hook carried the whole area** | `FacilityTables.groundY` was already `WorldCtx.ground` for the worktop (phase 2) | **adding the surface step to it moved actors, decals, the slime trail, `PathLine`, `TacticalGrid` and the cursor pick with NO other edit. The spill quads and the mast/plant/bench instances are the only things that needed a `y` of their own, because none of them goes through that hook** |
| ^ the kerb is the city's, extracted to `render/world/Kerb.hx` | segment collect + mitred emit, called `(BW, BT, H, 0)` by `Ground` and `(BW, BT, 0, −KERB_DROP)` by the facility — the two differ only in which of the pair moved | **the miter is NOT optional here: the park is *enclosed* by walkway, so its four corners are concave and two unmitred bands at one y z-fight. Measured: **135 segments — 61 walkway→road** (the full 65-cell sidewalk less its 4-cell driveway) **and 74 walkway→grass**, plus **4 risers**, exactly the driveway. **0 wall slots** — no dropped surface touches masonry on a generated compound, so that branch is a guard that never fires** |
| ^ the paint needed no new art | lane lines and bays are geometry in the city's own `ground-road-paint.png`, one merged mesh | **the road band is found by its ROW EXTENT — straight, full width, no intersection, so none of `Ground`'s crossing machinery is needed — and takes dashed/double/dashed. A bay is struck wherever a LOT cell abuts a pavement apron, which IS the edge cars park at, so no rectangle has to be found. Gated on the coordinate ALONG that edge and not on `(col + row)`: the diagonal gate a lit line uses comes out stepped on a straight run** |
| ^ **the park is a layout now, and its path is plain WALKWAY on purpose** | the old pass was a flat per-cell 10/30/60 roll — even scrub with no shape, 42% of the lawn unwalkable | **a 2-cell path down the long axis arrives KERBED AND LIT for free, because the ground pass and `FacilityLamps`' lit-line table both key on a walkway boundary. Benches alternate sides every 5 cells; planting comes off 4 clump centres falling with distance. Generator-side, NEW AREAS ONLY, nothing migrates. Verified headlessly by generating throwaway `AreaGame`s into the live region and removing them (768 areas before and after): 4-5 benches on a 5-cell rhythm, planted cover **42% → 22%**, path a clean 2x30 run** |
| ^ ^ and that path needed a lamp CAP | with a path through it every grass cell down both sides borders a walkway and qualifies as a lit line — **16 candidates** on a generated area, which lines a lawn with street lamps | **`PARK_LAMPS` 4, taken row-major so which four is deterministic. The cap is on the GRASS row alone; the kerb and lot lines keep the spacing gate** |
| ^ `facility/bench-park`, the second prop with an AUTHORED FRONT | **94,576 → 2,772 tris at tex 352 = 44 texels/tri**, split 1.27x so the offline route worked, `dropMR` (metal max 255), `baseColor` a straight darken from atlas 86/89/90 | **the front is local **+z**, MEASURED: the backrest sits at z −0.163 off the built mesh, so `FacilityGround.facing` turns each bench at the walkway beside it — a bench facing the shrubbery is the whole failure mode. Two reference notes: `propstat` called the raw drop TOUCHING THE EDGE and it was not (`BG_TOL` 60 keys gpt's own vignette as subject, so the reported bbox was the whole canvas while the subject had 80 px of margin), and it arrived at subject p50 **46** against the 69-77 that bakes correctly — fixed with `--lift 73`, not a re-prompt** |
| **The kerb was drawn on two sides of four, and a winding bug under `DoubleSide` is INVISIBLE, not latent** | `Kerb.emit` winds its band off the direction of `a->b`, which `FacilityGround` supplies per cell edge. Measured headlessly, one cell, four edges: dir 0 (−z) and dir 3 (+x) come back with the top band's normal **DOWN** and the drop face pointing **inward**, both quads of the segment together | **so a kerb existed only on a surface's +z and −x sides, and what showed on the other two was `KERB_DROP` 0.2 of unglazed slit under the walkway lip reading as a **black strip**. `Ground` never showed it: its border mesh is `DoubleSide`, which flips the normal in the fragment shader, while every `FacilityGround` mesh is `FrontSide`. Fix is four lines in `Kerb.push` — swap the endpoints when `(bx−ax)*nz − (bz−az)*nx < 0`. Endpoint SET unchanged, so the miter's `vkey` join is untouched; UV runs along `a->b` so a reversed segment just tiles the other way. City unchanged** |
| **All four park lamps stood on the park's rim with their heads aimed OUT of it** | `{ on: GRASS, by: WALKWAY }` — and a compound's pavement RINGS the lawn (measured: 120 grass↔walkway edges, all against walkway), so the rim qualifies as readily as the path. Row-major + a hard cap of 4 spent every lamp before reaching the path: replayed against the real `borders()`, `[5,19] [11,19] [2,22] [13,23]` | **`FacilityModel.isParkPath` is the missing predicate: a walkway cell whose RUN across one axis has grass at BOTH ends, capped at `PATH_MAX` 4 cells or a scan crosses the compound and calls the main sidewalk a path. The generator writes the path as plain `TEMP_WALKWAY` (which is what made it arrive kerbed and lit for free), so there is no id to test and no rect in `generatorInfo`. Verified live: exactly the 50 cells (7,19)..(8,43), the whole 2x25 path and nothing else** |

Correct, not coincidence: **a pane is a pane**, so a shorter opening is fewer of them and not smaller ones** |
| ^ **AN ART'S OUTERMOST TEXEL ROW IS NOT RELIABLY OPAQUE, and `alphaTest` then DELETES it** | reported as a thin slit along the top of every 2-cell window with the interior showing through — *"its just a little too low… increase scale a little?"*. Dumped the built art's top rows: **row 0 comes back at alpha 4.4** against `addPane`'s `alphaTest` 0.02 * 255 = **5.1**, so the row is discarded and the glass is one texel short of its opening. A property of the CUT, not of this drop: the 3-pane art's top row happens to land opaque (254), which is the only reason it never showed | **`WINDOW_OVERLAP` **0.025** — the pane quad is drawn 1.25% bigger at each edge, **in BOTH axes so the art keeps its aspect and nothing is stretched**, grown along the run's own axis (never across, which moves the quad off the glazing slab's face instead of lengthening it). 0.051 units = **two texel rows** at either art's density; one closes the slit, two also cover the bilinear tap on its neighbour. Invisible because the glazing slab is `GLASS_T` 1 inside a `WALL_T` 2 wall, so the overhang is buried in masonry. The head and sill caps keep the opening's TRUE size — only the glass grows** |
| ^ measure an art's aspect against the LOADER's own alpha threshold, not a convenient one | `Textures.loadAlphaCropped` skips alpha <= 8; measuring the source at a stricter cut gave 1.972 | **read back off the LIVE material it crops to **316x161 = 1.963** (against the 3-pane art's 464x160 = 2.900), so `WINDOW_ASPECT_2` carries that. A third of a percent = a 0.01-unit stretch, but there is no reason to hold the wrong number. Live: pane materials read 464x160 / 316x161 / 464x160 / 316x161, `repeat` 1 and `offset` 0 on all four; pane meshes **2 → 4**, both windowed structures carrying both lengths** |

Raycast down it (x 910, y 250-430) came back `roof.png` at y 6.0 for five consecutive samples between two `booth-roof` hits at 7.35 | **five parts per band, not one: **both OUTER faces run to the corner and meet in a line, both INNER faces stop one thickness short**, so the square is enclosed. Shrinking it to a bounded 1.1 did stop that and **created the reported mismatch instead** — a 13° pencil standing in the middle of the wide circle the SpotLight still throws, because `LAMP_LIGHT.angle` is shared and a shaft exists to OUTLINE that circle | **landed — a mast stands in the open and takes a full shell; a fitting bolted to a wall has its light cut off flat by the masonry, so the half that would be inside the building is simply not built. `LightConeOpts` gains `arc` + per-instance `yaws`; the wedge is built centred on local +z (`thetaStart = -arc/2`) and yawed to the outward normal, so nothing has to know how three numbers its theta. Verified over every vertex of every instance: extent along the outward normal is **[0, 3.106]** — a hard 0, so the shell can never cross the axis plane, at any height and for any radius** |

Measured on every hood: cut plane at **−0.02** off the masonry, cone top at **y 4.75** inside the hood's 4.5–5.0 band** |
| ^ the flat cut is left OPEN, not capped | a face there is a second additive surface stacked flat on the wall | **it would read as a bright band up the masonry. `openEnded` already covers it** |

A door now has a hood, a lens, a half cone and a pooled spotlight, so a flat trapezoid under the same lamp is a SECOND light in the same place, drawn from a different height with an unrelated shape — and what actually separates a window is that a window has no fitting: it lights the ground by BEING A HOLE, which is exactly what the quad draws | **landed — the whole door branch is now one `door()` call. Partition verified live and it is 54/54 clean: **27 masts and 8 door lamps OUTDOOR, 19 ceiling fixtures INDOOR**** |
| **A CONTACT SHADOW IS A FIELD, NOT A SET OF QUADS** (supersedes the five rows below) | reported as "wall corner shadow is just missing producing a visible line", and the same beside doors. | ^ **ANYTHING THAT MUST NOT BE SEEN THROUGH THE VISION MASK IS APPLIED AT OR AFTER `<fog_fragment>`, KEYED ON THE MASK** | the field version ALSO shipped broken, reported as **holes in the LOS shadow**. `VisionMask` does not darken a hidden surface, it MIXES IT TOWARD THE FOG COLOUR so the hidden region "adds no new silhouette to the frame" (its own header). A term applied upstream — the first cut scaled `irradiance` at `<lights_fragment_end>` — survives into the veil at the floor weight `MASK.hidden` **0.14**, so the wall footprint printed through as blotches at ~6% modulation, AND it shaded floor the player cannot see | **`ContactMask` multiplies `gl_FragColor.rgb` at `<fog_fragment>`. Measured: visMaskRaw exactly **1.0 visible, 0 hidden**** |
| ^ ordering is bought by the NESTING, not by the anchor | both hooks replace `<fog_fragment>` with itself plus their code, so whoever patches FIRST lands LAST in `main()` | **`addFloor` is `VisionMask.patch(ContactMask.patch(mat))`; swap it and the shader reads an unassigned global. Profile across a wall after the fix: peak 215 mid-slab, **~40% darkening at the masonry edge**, ramp dead by 1.6 world units — peak is 215 not 255 because a 2-unit wall is 12 texels against a 4.2-texel blur, so a THIN wall occludes less, which is the right way round** |
| **THE VISION MASK REVEALS A CELL, AND A FACILITY WALL IS HALF A CELL** | reported as "wide strips of undarkened floor outside the room before the LOS shadow starts", and NOT the contact pass — it predates the phase. `VisionMask.polygon` reveals a blocker as a WHOLE cell and its LOS segments sit on cell boundaries, which held while the only blockers were the tunnels' (`SewerGeom` fills a solid cell corner to corner). **A facility wall is the first blocker in the game thinner than the cell the mask grids it on** — `slab` centres `WALL_T = CELL/2`, so a quarter of every wall cell is open floor on each open side, and the far one is floor BEHIND the wall. Measured before, masonry face at cell 27.25: mask **50% at 27.001, 10% at 26.835** — a full world unit of near-white floor past the face. The contact field is no cover (9% at 1 unit, 2% at 1.5) | **new `VisionMaskOpts.blockerInset`, in cells, DERIVED and not tuned: `(CELL - WALL_T)/2 / CELL` = 0.25, i.e. `slab`'s own inset. Entry face is recovered from the ray's own hit against the cell bounds (`FACE_EPS`; segments are axis-aligned on integers) and ACCUMULATED over every ray into `sideAt`, never taken from the first — a corner pier is looked at from two sides at once** |

Put that clause FIRST on any future sheet — it costs nothing and a re-roll costs $0.057** |

| ^ **the cable's own lift is 0.64, the steepest in the file — and the thing to CHECK is the cross-section** | black rubber painted honestly came back at linear **0.0093** / srgb 23.9, a fifteenth of `wall-room` and `lift_gamma`'s hole failure at full strength | **0.64 lands 0.0394, just under the rack front's 0.0473. A gamma under 1 lifts the DARKS hardest, so it flattens exactly the shading that makes a flat strip read as round — measured after: **44.0 at the edges to 75.2 down the middle, still 1.71:1**, which survives at the ~13 device px a 0.18-unit ribbon draws** |
| ^ **A PARABOLA AND NOT A SPLINE — `PathLine`/`SlimeTrail` are the wrong shape to borrow** | both build exactly this ribbon (sample a centreline, offset sideways, widen to a strip, tile v) and both rebuild EVERY FRAME from a PATH of many cells | **a cable has TWO ANCHORS: straight chord, sag term, `4t(1-t)` taper (zero at both ends — `PathLine`'s taper reached from the other side), one sine rolled once off `mix` and baked. Nothing ticks. AND THE WIDTH AXIS AND THE SAG AXIS ARE THE SAME VECTOR BUT MUST NOT BE THE SAME VARIABLE: `w = n x t` winds the quads, so negating it turns every face into the masonry and it never appears — the sag rides that axis with its own SIGN, picked so a horizontal run droops and a vertical drop bows sideways** |
| ^ **EVERY ONE OF FOUR BUGS WAS FOUND BY A COUNT, NOT A SCREENSHOT** | the family shipped its first build at **52 panels and 0 runs**, with both halves broken at once — one counter could not have said so, and a run that did not emit looks exactly like a run off camera | **split the readout into panels/wired/runs. (1) `wired` 0: the greedy spacing deal essentially never lands on the one cell beside a machine — machines now claim their neighbour in a FIRST pass. (2) `links` 0: the span test borrowed the panels' own predicates wholesale. A bench between two panels is not a collision (2.0 tall, a clear unit off the slab face, so it OCCLUDES the cable, which is correct) and `hangs`' carries-both-ways clause is a plate's problem, not a cable's; a FITTING HEIGHT test against the run's own lowest point replaced a bare `m.deco > 0`, since half that family passes under. (3) `CABLE_SAG` as a fraction of span: fine on a 2-unit drop, **1.68 units** of droop on a 3-cell link, and worse every time the reach went up — it is world units now, because a strung cable's sag is set by its SLACK and not by how far apart its ends are** |
| ^ **and TWO knobs measured to be doing nothing** | reach 5 → 8 took candidate pairs **19 → 30** and produced **no extra runs at all** — what refuses a link is the masonry in between, not the distance | **a 1-in-3 skip roll was built to stop the compound reading as a diagram, then DELETED: only 4 of 30 candidates are eligible at all, so the roll was throwing away the scarce half of the family to manufacture the abundant one. Emptiness comes out of the geometry for free — glazing, doorways and tall fittings refuse most spans by themselves** |

| ^ **the sockets moving DOWN the wall changed the cable family, not just its height** | a run between two fittings at head height sags under gravity; between two sockets a foot off the ground there is nothing to sag INTO, and the old 0.45 droop is underground at mid-span | **a socket-to-socket run lies in the FLOOR plane and its whole offset is lateral — `CABLE_SNAKE` 0.55, a cable bowing out into the room and back, which reads far less mechanical than the sag did. `strip` took a `StripOpts` for it: the plane became a NORMAL (wall face or straight up) and the bow's sign became a HINT VECTOR the caller supplies, falling through to the seed when the chord is too nearly parallel to answer it. An exclusion says *not here* — a fire point is 2.0 wide, a quarter of a cell either side of its centre, and the only thing that cannot be shared is the FACE; a spacing says *not near anything of MINE* and only that is about rhythm. All three neighbours (`FacilityPoint.places`, `FacilityTables.runs`, `hugs`' corner rule) are pure functions of the grid, so none has to run first** |

Run it the other way and the piece comes back inside out. Two faults `check` caught: `sweep` built each ring's frame from a fixed up-vector, which FLIPS where the path crosses that vector's plane (the gooseneck's apex came back as two zero-area bowties — parallel-transport the frame), and a lathe took its v range from its first and last RING rather than its z EXTENT, so the mug's rim mapped past the top of its cell and came back wearing leaves** |

A mug on every desk plus one of the other three rolled, both at the BACK on the hand the tower is not on — the tower takes one half of the worktop and the keyboard the front of the other. `pools` lays a contact pool under each off the same table the batches are sized by, and `FacilityClutter` takes a keep-clear off each. **Cost: four batches, one texture** (14 / 5 / 3 / 6 on the measured area) — the price of four KINDS, against one glb holding all four in a fixed arrangement for one call and every desk dressed identically. **The plant is the honest failure**: Blender is worse at foliage than TRELLIS and an opaque leaf mass is a blob; it reads as a shrub at the game camera and would not survive a close-up** |
| **THE ROUND PROPS GO HAND-MODELLED, WITH NO DRAW CALL AT STAKE — 1,200 → 44 AND 5,710 → 284 TRIS** | the three drums and the gas rack already shipped as TRELLIS glbs, so both routes are one instanced batch per livery and the call line is flat. On trial: the triangle count and CONTROL OF THE PALETTE. `models-src/facility/drum_build.py`, a twelve-sided can (a measured floor — at `DECO_ROUND_D` 1.2 from eight metres up, eight show their flats and sixteen buys nothing) and four eight-sided bottles in a frame. All four `holes 0, coplanar 0` | **landed behind `DECO_DRUM_BLENDER`, both sets shipping. THE ART IS A WRAP AND NOT A BAKE: two pack4 drops whose cells are FLAT UNROLLED CYLINDER SKINS, u once round the barrel, each band its own 1/12 slice, seam at the back — so a livery is a CELL, where the generated rack needed a baseColor fit, a sat fit and two rounds of reported-from-the-game correction. Neither entry ships a colour knob. **Prompted against `style_prop` and not `style`** though they are tiling sheets: the clause is what matters, not the noun, and `style`'s desaturating line is how nine props here shipped grey. Also: an authored mesh HOLDS ITS ORIENTATION (`yaw0` 0, where the bake needed `Math.PI`), and `check` caught twenty z-fighting pairs from a frame welded FLUSH — uprights now 0.02 proud, 0.02 under the head rail, inset a `RAIL_T` at the ends** |
| **THE PRE-TILED CELL HAS A DENSITY BUDGET, AND THE SHELVING IS REFUSED ON IT** | the frame spends two labels, so the prize was one call — but its plates take `cap` at `DECO_SHELF_TILE` **0.55** over a 2.6 x 1.666 plate, i.e. **4.7 x 3.0 repeats**, and at `tex 512` a 2x2 sheet's cell is 256 px, so **54 px per repeat** against a perforated plate whose holes sit on a 24 px pitch | **NOT CONVERTED. The budget is `cell px / repeats` against the art's own FEATURE PITCH, and it is why the cabinet's 2.86-repeat flank is fine (plain metal, two rivets) and this is not. A family that tiles a FINE pattern at a period far under its own size cannot take this route without a texture bigger than the thing deserves** |
| **THE BOOKCASE AND THE BASIN LAND, AND A BASIN IS A HOLE** | bookcase: four boards proud of the spines (`BOARD_OUT` 0.03) running BETWEEN the stiles, 60 tris — a shelf was a painted stripe that caught no light. Basin: the lid inset, pulled to the bowl's own aspect and dropped 0.34, so the bowl is a real tapered recess with a rim, and the tap is two boxes instead of an alpha-cut sprite, 44 tris. Both `holes 0, coplanar 0` | **bookcase **136 → 134 calls, +132 tris**; basin **112 → 111, +128 tris** — one and not two because `B_BASIN_TOP` is SHARED with the benches and the partition cope and never goes empty. The basin's glb retires the family's only alpha-cut mesh. **The mirror stays a quad**: it hangs on the WALL at `MIRROR_Y` 3.4, so folding it in would have made the mesh 4.4 tall for `Models.instanced` to squash to `BASIN_H` — the batch takes a new `BASIN_MESH_H` instead, the mesh's own height, so the scale is 1. Trap: `inset_individual`'s `thickness` is NOT a flat per-edge offset, so the inner rect must be read back and scaled, not derived** |
| **THE MACHINE IS A GLB AND IT WON TRIANGLES TOO — MERGED GEOMETRY CANNOT BE CULLED PER UNIT** | third fitting down the route, `models-src/facility/machine_build.py`, **36 tris against the quads' 8**, 2.8 cube, z_min 0, `holes 0, coplanar 0`. Shape: the front art's three bands are three stacked boxes with the louvre one pulled back `GRILLE_IN` 0.035, so it gets a real ledge at each end — and the control strip does NOT move, because `machLights` stands five specks 0.03 proud of the front face inside it | **pinned frame, both arms in one session: quads **101 calls / 56,461 tris**, glb **99 / 56,437** — **−2 calls AND −24 tris**, the first arm here to win both. The quad build merges all 12 machines into three buffers and submits every one of their 96 triangles whenever any part is in frustum; the instanced batch culls per instance and this frame drew TWO. **On a SCATTERED family the triangle sign flips** — the "+n for the shape" line in the entries above holds only while the family is small and mostly visible** |
| ^ **ONE MESH AT BOTH HEIGHTS — the rack's trap the other way up** | `DECO_MACHINE_LOW_SZ` is not a COUNT of anything: the unit is a cube at either size and `machLights` reads fractions of h and of the face's own width, so `Models.instanced` scaling by height alone is exactly right here, where for the rack it was wrong because `DECO_RACK_LOW_H` means *three chassis* | **ASK WHAT THE SECOND SIZE MEANS before deciding whether it is a second mesh. Two batches off one path, one material, one program, one warm row — and all three sheet cells pack at 1x1, the only family here with no repeat to bake, because every face of it is `pane` or `localCap` at the unit's own width** |
| **THE CABINET IS A GLB TOO, AND THE SAVING IS `LABELS − MESHES`** | second fitting down the route and the first whose case is ONLY the call — a cabinet IS the axis-aligned slab the triage says to emit. `models-src/facility/cabinet_build.py`, **36 tris against the quads' 8**, 1.9611 x 3.432 x 1.225, z_min 0, `holes 0, coplanar 0`. Shape it does add: the two doors are proud LEAVES (24 of those tris), so their edges cast a reveal and the plinth band reads as a toe kick instead of paint | **A/B on one pinned frame, both arms in one session after a settle: quads **151 calls / 2,003,859 tris**, glb **149 / 2,003,943** — **−2 calls, +84 tris** (3 cabinets compound-wide, 24 → 108). TWO because its three labels are three whole buffers going empty at once, where the rack won only ONE because its three collapse into two batches. **Count `labels − meshes` before building anything**: one label and one mesh breaks even at best, which is the booth and the barrier again. Each leaf's front takes the SUB-RECT of the front cell its own art occupies (`buildlib.sub`, read off the image) and the carcass front behind carries the whole cell, so a sliver past a leaf is still the right pixels. `FacilityDeco.racks` → `fittings`, a `{kind, path, h, on}` table; rows stay with the flag off, because `FacilityArea.EXTRA` is index-aligned with `green`** |
| **THE SERVER RACK IS A GLB AND IT COST ONE CALL FEWER THAN THE QUADS** | the first room fitting down the Blender route, and the first result that inverts the booth/barrier verdict. `models-src/facility/rack_build.py` builds TWO objects, **156 and 96 tris against four quads' 8**, evaluated at 1.660 x 3.200 x 1.235 and 1.660 x 1.600 x 1.235, z_min 0. What the quads could not state: a rack is SIX CHASSIS in a frame, each behind its own bezel, on a skirted foot under an overhanging lid — and six is `DECO_RACK_H / (DECO_RACK_TILE / 3)`, not a style count | **A/B on one pinned free-cam frame, both arms in ONE session after a settle (`FacilityStyle.DECO_RACK_GLB`, a flag and not a deletion): quads **100 calls / 398,897 tris**, glbs **99 / 399,013** — **−1 call, +116 tris**. Compound-wide 5 tall + 12 low, 136 tris → 1,932. GPU median 6.36/7.60/8.39ms across builds and therefore meaningless, per the power-state rule** |
| ^ **SETTLE THE FRAME BEFORE SAMPLING — the roof reveal eases, and an A/B taken during it is luck** | the first pass at this A/B read 473,704 against 473,880 and agreed to within 176 only because both arms happened to be sampled at the same point in the ease; the identical camera reads **75k fewer triangles** once the reveal has finished | **wait ~3s (180 frames) after `lookFrom` before collecting, and check `min == max` over the sample window. Both arms in ONE session, because a rebuild is also a reload** |
| ^ **A CUBE HAS SIX FACES AND AN EMITTED FITTING HAS FOUR** | `FacilityDeco.unit` emits a front, two flanks and a lid and NO bottom (`FacilityRoof.box`: this camera never sees under a fitting). An assembly of `create_cube` boxes brings them all back, and three pairs landed exactly coplanar — carcass bottom against the skirt's AND against the floor plane, carcass top against the lid's, three backs in the masonry. Reported from the game as the top and bottom z-fighting | **deleting them cleared the fight (156 → 130 tris) and was WRONG — see the row below. Keep every face and pull the PLANES apart: `CARC_IN` 0.02 off the carcass head so the lid's top owns that plane, `TRIM_IN` 0.05 pulling the skirt and lid INSIDE the carcass front and back (flush fights the carcass, proud lands a hair in front of the chassis bezel, and 0.05 is what clears the top chassis's own lid 0.009 under the cap's). Floors exempt: nothing sees under a fitting** |
| ^ **A HOLE IS A FAULT TOO — a prop is a SOLID, however few of its faces the camera meets** | with the buried faces deleted both cabinets shipped watertight-failing, reported next and correctly. Every face that never faces the camera still BOUNDS the object, and a later pass that wants inside-from-outside (a shadow volume, a hull, an outline shell) pays for the hole | **`buildlib.check` runs on every object before export and reports holes (an edge not shared by exactly two faces) and CO-FACING faces closer than `near` **0.01** with overlapping ground — NEAR-coplanar, because 0.005 shimmers as readily as 0. Pairs sharing a vert skipped (an inset ring is four coplanar faces that tile), -z skipped whole. Both racks `holes 0, coplanar 0`; it caught the chassis/cap pair before the game did. **130 → 156 and 76 → 96 tris, paid knowingly**. Run it on every hand-modelled prop from here** |
| ^ **a packed cell CANNOT WRAP, so a tiling emitter's repeat has to go into the SHEET** | `localFace` divides by `DECO_RACK_TILE` on both axes, so a flank carries 0.75 across its depth by **2.0** up its height; `buildlib.unwrap` maps a cell 0..1 over the whole face and stretched the square flank art **2.67x**, reported as stretched sides | **`tools/modelsheet.py --repeat`: each cell is tiled and cropped to `u x v` repeats BEFORE being scaled in, values under 1 being a crop. Same density as the quads, with no geometry change and no UV change. This is the general answer for any hand-modelled prop replacing a TILING emitter** |
| ^ **one mesh at two heights is wrong when a CONSTANT says how many of something it holds** | `DECO_RACK_LOW_H` is `DECO_RACK_TILE` — exactly three chassis — and `FacilityDeco.lights` deals `h / DECO_RACK_TILE * DECO_LED_PER_TILE` rows off that same arithmetic, so the six-chassis mesh drawn low gave **six squashed units under three rows of lights** | **`Models.instanced` scales by HEIGHT alone and there is no way round it: the low cabinet is a second object out of the same script, and a second path, material, program and warm row. Check what ELSE reads a family's constants before reusing one mesh at two sizes** |
| ^ **WHY it wins the call: the PER-FACE TEXTURE RULE is the thing a glb collapses** | `3d-geometry.md` requires one texture per FACE, so a rack's front, flanks and lid are three labels = **three merged meshes = three calls** for the whole compound; the glb wears ONE material and is **two** instanced batches (one per height, off one path — `Models.instanced` scales by height alone) | **the booth (7,431 → 34) and the barrier (3,098 → 22) are not contradicted: both were ONE-texture objects, where a glb can only add triangles. **The test this adds is how many TEXTURES the emitted version spends** — at three, a glb starts a call ahead before any shape argument is made. `tools/modelsheet.py` packs the three BUILT labels, so each one's fitted `lift` is already in the pixels and the entry carries no baseColor/lift/sat** |
| ^ **the skirt and the lid take NO HEIGHT, and that is not a style call** | `FacilityDeco.lights` puts a rack's status specks at `h * (r + 0.5) / rows` off the same chassis count, so the first build — which spent 0.17 of the height on a base and a lid — moved every chassis up to **a sixth of its own height** away from its own lights | **they stand PROUD instead: total exactly `DECO_RACK_H`, rows exactly `RACK_TILE / 3`, the LED pass untouched and outside the switch. A hand-modelled prop that replaces emitted geometry inherits every OTHER pass keyed on the same constants — check what else reads them before spending height** |
| facility | **A ROUND ALPHA RAMP IS A SPECK'S, AND A MONITOR IS NOT A SPECK** | reported as a bright spot on the screen and "a bad attempt of bloom". It is not the bloom: the screen's additive quad rides `FacilityDeco`'s LED mesh, whose alpha map is `makeRectGlowGradient(0.30)` — a soft ROUND dot, right for a 0.12 status speck and an OVAL over a 1.7 x 1.25 panel. The previous pass felt it and reached for the wrong knob twice, fitting `WORK_SCREEN_LEVEL` down 0.35 → 0.10 and pulling `GLOW_W/H` in to 0.78/0.72 so the ellipse would not touch the bezel | **collapse the quad's uv onto (0.5, 0.5) — four numbers.** The SDF reads 255 across its whole interior, so the panel lights EVENLY and its own rectangle is the shape, which is what a screen IS; zero derivative lands it on mip 0 and the mesh stays one call. With no ellipse to keep off the bezel `GLOW_W/H` went back out to the art's measured content 0.93/0.875 and the level stayed at its fit. **An alpha ramp is part of a quad's SHAPE, and a shared additive mesh hands every tenant the same one — a rectangular tenant opts out for free** |
| ^ **A DIVIDED ROOM IS TWO ROOMS TO A LIGHT FITTING** | the washroom's one bulb sat on top of its separator, every time. `FacilityLamps` lattices a room by its RECT, a washroom is under `ROOM_SPACING` on both axes so `nx`/`ny` fall to 1, and `toiletPartition` puts the T's **stem** on `room.y1 + h/2` — so the single point is always the one cell in the room that is a wall | **`divider(m, r)` asks the finished grid: the `FIT_PARTITION` cell NEAREST the centre (the stem, never the bar), its run direction read by COUNTING neighbours per axis (`rectOf`'s own rule — at the T's junction one x neighbour stands against two in z), then force the lattice to 2 ACROSS it.** The existing `(j + 0.5)/n` spacing does the rest; a point still on the divider is nudged to a neighbour. 2 bulbs where there was 1, every other room unmoved since `max(n, 2)` is a no-op above `ROOM_SPACING` |
| ^ **A PIECE BURIED IN ANOTHER MUST CLEAR ITS OWN HALF-THICKNESS, not merely the surface** | the mug's handle came back out through the glaze: the 192° arc put each end at x 0.1067 against a wall at 0.1244, buried 0.018 where the **rod's radius is 0.022**, so the far side of the end cap re-emerged as a facet welded to the side. Two more of the same shape in one turn — the basin's shaft cap at z 1.73 sat ABOVE the bowl's own floor at 1.200 and cut across the sink (probed as face #10, local y 1.73); the plant's 22° skirt reached the pot rim's radius at z 0.358 against a rim top of 0.360 | **216° buries the handle 0.040/0.055 (and 6 sides → 8, +28 tris: at this size the rod is the second-biggest thing in the silhouette); the basin's shaft is DELETED outright, since the vessel is one closed lathe and the opening is covered by the flange all round; the plant's tiers go 78/50/22 → 80/62/44 for 0.11 of daylight over the rim, which stands it up from 0.6164 to 0.5146 across and took `WORK_PLANT_W` 0.64 → 0.53 with it** |
| ^ **A LATHE'S FIRST RING IS NOT A BOTTOM, and capping it by reflex puts a lid on the vessel** | the basin came back with a SECOND polygon across it after the shaft went, and this one was the bowl's own: `buildlib.tube` closes a ring with a full DISC, and the profile's first ring is the flange's outer edge — the widest point of the whole vessel at 1.25 x 0.775 — so the cap is a 20-gon at **z 1.806, 0.07 BELOW the rim at 1.876**. Largest horizontal face in the model (area 3.842 vs the bowl floor's 0.346), and the previous pass **looked straight at it in a face dump and let it through because its normal points DOWN** — the material is `doubleSided`, and a face nothing can be behind is not harmless either way | **`turned` takes `cap_lo`; the bowl passes False.** Every vessel here runs OUT and back IN over itself (the mug's coffee, the pot's soil, this), so the first ring is a MOUTH, not a base. A drop-in sink is open at that edge by construction — it is where the vessel meets the worktop, 0.006 over a lid that overhangs it 0.13 all round — and what a flange underside wants there is an ANNULUS a one-loop lathe cannot state and nothing can see. `check` reports holes 24 (4 counter lid + 20 flange edge) by design; 810 → 792 tris |
| ^ **A Rec.709 SAT MIX HOLDS LUMA BY CONSTRUCTION, so a BRIGHT prop needs a second knob** | the drum-rad entry above wrote down the wrong lesson — "the brightness comes out with it" is true only when the brightness sits in ONE dominant channel. Yellow is two at 210 and 172: measured, `sat 0.61` lands drum-bio's chroma on the family (73.6) and leaves its luma at **174.6**, against rad's 92.5 and steel's 60.1. Same fault on the desk mug, whose cream glaze is **linear 0.5548** — 4.4x the laminate it stands on and the brightest interior surface in the game | **`sat` fits the HUE, `baseColor` fits the VALUE, and the pair is solved together because each moves the other's metric.** drum-bio `sat 0.85` + `bc 0.33` → chroma 74.9 / luma 103.6, verified on the BUILT glb at 75.1 / 104.0; desk-mug `sat 0.5` + `bc 0.40` → 6.0 / 125.8; desk-plant `sat 0.5` and **no** `baseColor`, because both its cells were already in the value band and only the pot's hue was out — which is the test for one knob or two. A chroma read off the sRGB source over-predicts the linear mix on a channel near zero: 0.38 was the first fit and landed at 42, half the family |
| ^ **A SLOT'S TENANT SHOULD NOT ALWAYS BE THE SAME OBJECT** | "cup appears way too often — should be random clutter", plus the cabinet having no lid dressing. `WORK_MUG_PCT` 70 was chosen when the front slot had no other claimant; it has one now, so the slot fills either way and the number only decides how often the thing in it is the SAME model. And `FacilityClutter.slots` excluded the cabinet on a premise that was simply false — the note called it "chest-high with a rounded top", `cabinet_build.py` builds a plain chamfered box | **70 → 30, and the cup itself becomes THREE GLAZES OFF ONE MESH — the hazard barrels' arrangement, `desk-mug-b`/`-c` re-entering `desk-mug.glb` with their own `baseColor` tint and `sat`.** `Models.instanced` is keyed by PATH and carries no per-instance colour, so a glaze is a batch either way and produced this way it costs no art, no triangles and no Blender pass; all three land at luma ~116-126 and differ only in hue (6.0 neutral / 12.3 cool / 23.0 warm). Cabinet lid confirmed by scanning the merged mesh's vertex levels: **y 3.447** = `DECO_CABINET_H` + `CLUTTER_Y` |
| ^ **THE MIRROR WAS BLUE BECAUSE THE ROOM'S OWN LIGHT IS BLUE, third time** | "bright blue, which is ridiculous" — and the art was doing what its prompt asked, *"a cool light blue-white sheet"*. `FacilityStyle` already carries the warning: the installation is `LAMP_COLOR` 0x9db4d4, so a blue-white SURFACE under a blue-white LIGHT is not cool, it is saturated | **re-rolled solo (a mirror maps 0..1 with a frame, so it can never come off a shared sheet) with the fog specified perfectly neutral — measured chroma 2.2. Value refitted from scratch and INVERTED: the new art is 0.1141 linear, UNDER `wall-wash`'s 0.1593, so the lift BRIGHTENS for the first time on this entry (0.73, where the old art needed 2.05 to come down). Landed 0.1969 = 1.24x the wall, where the old roll was 1.67x and read as glowing. State a blue accent by VALUE and leave the hue to the lighting** |
| ^ **A PREDICATE ONE PASS OWNS IS A PREDICATE EVERY PASS NEEDS** | a desk in a room corner kept its monitor, tower and keyboard with nobody able to sit at it: `FacilityWork.chairs` refused the seat and `at()` did not, so the family disagreed with itself. Same cells were "half done missing polygons" — `wallHand` takes the FIRST wall, so two corner desks back onto different walls, are thinned on different axes by `DESK_D`, and the face loop skipped the panel between them because the neighbour `isTable` | **the seat test moves INTO `at()`, the pure function the other seven passes are built on, and the freed worktop falls back to `FacilityClutter`'s paperwork: 13 desk cells → 10 workstations, every one with a seat. `openHand` closes the gap — a face is closed by its neighbour only when NEITHER rect was cut away from the line they share. A run test may only skip a face when both rects actually reach it** |
| ^ **A DIVIDER STOPPED AT ITS OWN CELL BOUNDARY, AND SIGHT WENT STRAIGHT THROUGH IT** | reported as the washroom separator not reaching the outer wall, and separately as not blocking LOS. `FacilityGeom.slab` insets a wall cell by `(CELL - thick)/2` = 1.0, so masonry stands a unit back inside its cell and every wall-hugging family measures off that FACE — `rectOf`'s partition branch was the one that did not. And the divider was transparent in both layers: a partition cell finalises to a plain unwalkable id, `paintRoomFloor` rewrites it to marble+16, and BOTH are `TILE_SEETHROUGH = 1` | **rect extended to the neighbour's slab face, tested `isWall && carries` (a light well is glazing and still masonry; a DOOR answers `carries` and is not a wall) — measured gap **0.000** over every partition end that meets masonry. Sight answered on the OBJECT, `Decoration.canSeeThrough` false for `FIT_PARTITION` alone: the tile route would have made every rack, cabinet and machine opaque, since `placeFitting` stamps that id for all of them. Old saves need no migration — `recalcTile` re-derives on load** |
| ^ **A CELL WHOSE ID IS A FITTING HAS TO BORROW ITS FLOOR** | a washroom's drain drew one square of laboratory tile in the middle of the marble. `floorBuf` routes `Surf.GRATE` to `default: floors[Surf.TILE]`, right in a lab and wrong everywhere else — the generator writes the grate id BEFORE `paintRoomFloor`, and that pass steps over a final id | **`surfUnder` inherits the first non-`GRATE` indoor neighbour's surface, five lines, pure function of the grid, no new buffer and no new call. `GRATE` still refuses to be painted cell-wide (a 2 m square of grating reads as a vehicle pit) — the drain's own art is a centred decal and what belongs under it is the room's floor** |
| ^ **A SWEPT HANDLE HAS TWO CLEARANCES AND THE INVISIBLE ONE IS VERTICAL** | the mug was reported a second time as going into the cup, and the previous fix (192 → 216 degrees, so the end CAP stops emerging through the glaze) was right about the wrong axis. Measured on the mesh: the upper end at z 0.2815 puts the rod's CROWN at 0.3035 against a BREW cone standing at 0.2966 over that radius — 0.007 of rod lying in the coffee | **arc lowered and pushed out (centre 0.126/0.165, radii 0.105/0.100): crown 0.282 against the brew's 0.297, ends buried 0.046 and 0.031 against the rod's own 0.022, belly 0.081 clear of a 0.150 wall. 424 tris, `holes 0 / coplanar 0`, propstat 0 smears** |
| ^ **A SWATCH WITH A WIDTH IS A SWATCH WITH EDGES — and `lathe_uv` lays its left against its right** | the basin came back with a black seam and warped neighbours. NOT the wrap code (its `+1.0` push is correct): `R_CERAMIC` was a 15 x 20 px crop falling 15 counts of luma across those 15 px (89/86/83 at sd 4.2, 77 at its right edge) with BLACK texels 3 px past it, which mips bleed straight into the seam quad | **collapsed to ONE TEXEL at (309, 692) — measured as the crop's own mean exactly — the litter bin's finding applied to the piece that needed it most. Bowl u now spans 0.30189–0.30288, no width for a seam to be across. Same pass: the low rack's CARCASS FRONT wore the whole three-chassis cell behind chassis that cover its full height (and at half density on the tall cabinet, where the cell maps 0..1 whatever `h` is) — it takes the plain side swatch now, and every front-facing triangle of both cabinets was re-classified by the rect its UVs land in to prove it** |
| ^ **THE GENERATED SIBLING'S `sat` DOES NOT TRANSFER — read the LEVEL and the hue together** | "gas rack — reduce saturation". The hand-modelled rack was the only coloured prop in the family carrying NEITHER knob: its amber is a cell of an authored sheet, shipped raw at chroma 152.1 against a drum family fitted to 72.8–74.9. Its generated predecessor rejected `sat 0.5` as "almost white" — but that bake sits on a flat `baseColor 0.95`, and a desaturated BRIGHT surface is a pale one where a desaturated mid one is grey | **`sat 0.6` and the fit measured on the BUILT atlas rather than predicted: bottle cell 159.4/104.0/82.9, luma 114.2, **chroma 76.5**, on the drums' own band. No `baseColor` — nothing reported it as bright, and the Rec.709 mix in linear light holds luma by construction** |
| ^ **A BAG OF INDICES IS WHAT A RARITY TABLE LOOKS LIKE BEFORE IT HAS WEIGHTS** | "lamps are spawned too often too" — the desk lamp, and it had never been tuned at all: `1 + (g >>> 14) % 3` over plant/lamp/tray under `WORK_TRINKET_PCT 55` puts one on ~18% of every workstation desk | **`BIG = [1, 1, 3, 3, 6, 6, 2]`, which drops the lamp to ~8% and is also how the new journals entered without moving anything else. One line, no new constant, and the same shape `MUGS` already had** |
| ^ **A CORRIDOR PROP CANNOT BE A FITTING, so the cheap route is the only route** | "create blender broom and bucket and add them to corridors and toilets", plus a bin for the washroom. A fitting costs a `_FittingKind`, one of three free entities64 frames, a `_DecoPlace` row, a `selfCheck` tag, three `FacilityDeco` rows and a generator call site — and the generator stamps `deco` inside `_Room`s ONLY, so a fitting could not have reached half the ask | **`FacilityJanitor`, the compound's sixth render-only unstamped pass, on `FacilityKit`'s shape and gated `Surf.LINO` + `Surf.WASH` where the lab kit takes `Surf.TILE` — the interior divides by SURFACE, with no shared claim list and no ordering between builders. The washroom's bin came free: `indoorBins` needed `Surf.WASH` plus the fitting/worktop guards a washroom needs and a corridor does not. **A STRIDE LATTICE MUST BE COPRIME WITH ITS STEP**: `(col * 5 + row * 2) % 5` is `(row * 2) % 5` and selected whole ROWS of corridor — 7 candidates against 20** |
