NOT the checkpoint's trade — those objects were already props, and these are cheaper because the numbers are STATED: `z = r^2/(4f)` at **f/D 0.40**, so the horn stands at the focus and `MAST_H` is solved to land h on 3.6 exactly (a short glb is scaled UP and its plan grows). Shell = one lathe, one solid, vessel rule; both faces mapped RADIALLY because their art IS a disc; **5 x 20 bands cost what 6 x 16 did** and 16 sides read as a polygon. A RAISED COWL is how a fan well recesses without a boolean, and the guard is a PLATE (a flat polygon is a boundary edge all round). Louvre cell takes a **0.12 v-window** — 114 slats per cell is 95 per world unit laid whole. THE VALUE FIT GOES ON THE DOMINANT FACE: a 0.030 sheet mean read **54.7 / 55.1** in the built glbs against the bakes' 45.3 / 47.6, re-fitted to **50.2 / 50.5**; the gen's dish face was 11.7x the deck** |

| models | **ONE TEXEL DENSITY PER PROP: `unwrap(scale=)`** | both authored roof props were reported back from the game face by face — the flank at the "wrong u,v scale", the rim "different on each segment", a foot not "aligned to center" | **ONE AUDIT ANSWERED ALL OF IT: texels per world unit p10/median/p90/max was **0 / 161 / 1,380 / 3,134** on the condenser and **34 / 95 / 2,058 / 4,841** on the dish, with **80 and 38 zero-area triangles**; after, both read **80 / 95 / 97 / 181 and zero**. Three faults, all in `buildlib` and all now fixed there, so every future hand-modelled prop gets this free: (1) the aspect fit is isotropic but NOT CONSISTENT — its window is all the art there is, so the density is `rectW / faceW` and every face gets its own; `scale` is `uvfit`'s contract for a FLAT face. (2) `reps` floored a copy low — at `reps` = the turn count each face spans exactly 1.0, `atan2` of an exact multiple is 1e-9 short, `reps` multiplies that to 1e-7 past the old 1e-9 guard, and all 20 cowl facets clamped to u 1.0; the MIDPOINT of `[k, k+1]` is `k + 0.5`. (3) the fit's "UP goes on v" read `ext[2] > ext[oth]`, which agrees with `|n.z| < 0.5` on every axis-aligned face and fails the moment a piece is YAWED — a foot at 120° measured (0.15, 0.26, 0.05) and mapped a vertical face over (x, y), a line in plan. Plus: the louvre's hand-cut window was fitted to a MISMEASURED sheet (claimed 114 slats per cell, the row profile's dominant period is 34 px = **15**), and `mirror` folds at t 0 and t 0.5 so a seam off the `1 / n` grid puts both folds mid-face — `ring` snaps it** |

| roof | **THE DISH'S YOKE CAME OUT OF ITS PAINTED FACE** | reported as the centre strut going into the dish itself; plates 0.34 deep reaching 0.10 above the axle, with the reflector's back sitting ON the elevation axis | **`HUB` stands the assembly **0.26 along the boresight** off the axle, `YOKE_T` drops to 0.18 and a hub boss bridges the gap: the plate corner that reaches furthest forward (its -y, top one, at `cos(ELEV) * YOKE_T / 2 + sin(ELEV) * overshoot` = 0.153) now clears the back's 0.21 by **0.058**, against `check`'s 0.01. `MAST_H` re-solved for the standoff and the **`+0.0321` fudge deleted — it was exactly `SHELL * sin(ELEV)`**, the rim's forward edge being at `DEPTH` and not `DEPTH + SHELL`. h lands on 3.6 exactly either way, which it has to: a short glb is scaled UP and its plan grows. 948 → 888 tris (the feet's 6 mm arrises went with the degenerate faces), values re-audited **49.6 / 50.2 srgb**, no retone** |

Pinned pose: **calls 167 → 167, tris 293,430 → 293,298**, both families in buffers the roof already had** |

Now a 0.3 curb with a 0.1 lid on a **9 cm lip** — a lid flush with its curb is a stub whatever it is clad in — in `BOOTH_ROOF`'s painted steel decking, the one art in the compound that already IS a lid, at 0.69 units a pan so a 2.4 lid shows three and a half. Zero new art, one texture for the whole housing, and the isolator boxes share its buffer so they cost no draw call** |

| models | **A FACE'S OWN FRAME, NOT A PAIR OF WORLD AXES** | the dish's three feet reported a third time — two of the three not "aligned to center" like the one that looked right | **`unwrap`'s aspect fit picked a pair of WORLD axes, which is exactly right square to the grid and nonsense the moment a piece is YAWED: a 0.30 x 0.13 foot turned 120° measures **(0.26, 0.32)** in x and y, so u went on y and the art ran DIAGONALLY across it. `buildlib.frame(f)` instead — world up projected into the plane wherever the face has any, the face's own LONGEST EDGE on u where it does not, signs pinned on the flat branch so two tops of one piece cannot come out 180° apart. All three feet now take the identical rect, **du 0.05595 / dv 0.02425**, the one the 0° foot had; texels/unit spread **2.4x → 1.7x** on the dish, 1.9x on the condenser, zero degenerate either. **AXES 7** fell out of it: the frame with the rect taken WHOLE, for a PRE-CUT band — the axis pairs cannot do that job, axes 1 is (x, y) and a vertical face has no extent in one of them** |

| roof | **THE DISH'S STRUTS ENDED 4.5 CM SHORT OF THE HORN, FOUR TIMES OVER** | reported as a hole between the strut and the horn "for all four lines", against a comment claiming both ends were buried | **they stopped at 0.12 of R off the axis — 0.1373 out, where the horn's cone measures **0.064**. The near end was no better: 0.02 into a 0.05 shell leaves **0.0005** once the cap's tilt is counted (a 53° cap on a 0.028 ring spans 0.045 in z, and the mesh's front face is a CHORD between two rings so it sits ABOVE the paraboloid). `STRUT_IN 0.03` at `FOCUS + HORN_L * 0.25` inside a 0.0843 cone, `STRUT_THRU 0.04` out through the back where a real strut foot bolts. `run()` returns both — **horn 0.0262, thru 0.0182** — so neither can drift back. 888 tris, holes 0, coplanar 0** |

| roof | **THE CLUTTER CAP WAS FITTED AGAINST THE DROP, NOT AGAINST THE DECK** | reported as the roof clutter having albedo issues again, with a puddle reading almost right from one angle and dark from another, and: what are we reflecting? | **NOTHING — `FacilityRoof.pack` is Lambert with no envMap and `PropEnv` is indoor-only. It is albedo, and the number was in the manifest: `cap: 0.1` is **3.6x** the deck these lie on (`facility/roof` 0.0275), fitted against the raw drop's 0.15..0.36 instead. Measured on the built sheet: **19 cells at 0.0976..0.0978**, all of them the cap's own ceiling, against a tar patch at 0.0194; sheet mean **2.43x** the deck, cell-to-cell spread **7.6x**, brightest p95 **5.7x**. The photographed puddle is one cell whose art is a dark pool with a bright rim (p05 0.032, p95 0.133) — so the rim read as a white ring wherever it survived the mip and the fill as a hole where it did not, which is BOTH halves of the report from one cause. `cap: 0.03`, the deck's own value: **52/64 capped, mean 1.05x, spread 2.0x, p95 3.4x**. One field, no new art, and the cap can only darken so the tar cells are untouched** |

| roof | **NOTHING DRAWN TWICE: A SHARED TRUNK AND A LAY PER WAY** | reported as a z-fight on all three ways of the new trunking | **an L per unit means two units in one group lay THE SAME QUAD along `x = bx + o`, and each L covers its own corner square twice. Probed over the live geometry by intersecting every coplanar pair: **88 quads, 84 overlapping pairs, 1.67 square units**. `runs` now builds the group's whole network as `Seg` records and emits at the end (the fault is two quads on one patch of deck and that cannot be seen one quad at a time): ONE trunk per way spanning the box and every unit's row, a spur each stopping **half a cable short** of the trunk's centre line so the joint closes on its width, and `seg()` merging a new segment with EVERYTHING on its line rather than the first match. What no clipping can remove is a spur crossing another way's trunk — the ways are parallel — so each way lies one `ROOF_CABLE_LAY 0.02` higher than the last. **64 quads, 0 pairs, 0 area**, and fewer quads than before** |

| roof | **THE CONDENSER DOES CAST — ITS OWN PLAN HIDES THE SHADOW** | reported as not all roof structures throwing shadows, the condenser in particular | **measured before touching anything: the deck's light is **66% moon** (directional off took a pinned deck window rgb 2.2/6.9/17.8 → 0.1/3.0/6.0), `castShadow` IS on, the batch IS inside the moon's 180-unit box — but the moon stands **48° up** and the unit is **2.78 x 1.77 on the plan under 2.2 of height**, so its whole shadow falls under itself. Killing that batch's `castShadow` changed **7,632 px by a mean of 1.1 of 255**. Only the 3.6-tall dish on a 1.5 pedestal clears itself. So: `FacilityRoof.pool`, `FacilityDetail.pooled`'s rounded-box field under every condenser, dish, hatch and box, a fourth per-deck mesh in `Shell.roof` — **1 draw call a deck**. THE WEIGHT IS IN THE RAMP AND NOT ON THE MATERIAL: `FacilityArea.apply` SETS opacity off the deck's ease every frame, so `makeRectGlowGradient` took a `peak`. `roof.pools` must equal ac+dish+hatches+boxes — live **26 = 15+2+2+7**** |

| roof | **A HATCH IS A BOX AND IT WENT TO BLENDER ANYWAY** | reported twice on the same two objects — a new blender box with unique tex for the hatch, and "DON'T REUSE TEXTURES" for the isolator box; both wore `BOOTH_ROOF` at `ROOF_TILE 9.0` | **THE BOX-VS-SILHOUETTE TEST CANNOT SETTLE THIS ONE. A hatch IS a box and ten quads cost no draw call — but a merged mesh takes a TILING texture on a WORLD-ALIGNED UV, and at 9.0 each face got a ninth of a kiosk roof's standing seam. A 1.2 x 1.0 m lid's whole read is the tread plate ON it at a 51°-86° down camera, and a plate pattern is a PICTURE of a lid: it maps 0..1 onto that one face, which a glb's unwrap does and a tiling emitter cannot. The route was decided by whether the ART can be world-aligned, not by the outline. **56 tris** (2.58 x 2.18 x 0.4) and **88** (0.81 x 0.61 x 0.9), check clean, built AT the height their batch scales by. A fresh 1k sheet each, four cells for four surfaces, toned to **47.9 / 47.3 srgb** off drops at 0.078..0.150 linear; built glbs **49 / 49 srgb, 0 smears**. Curb and drip band take PRE-CUT bands mapped whole (axes 7) — a 0.3 face over a 2.58 SCALE is 11.6% of the cell and a centred crop lands on plain field where the fixing row is at row 465 of 512. Pinned pose against the same build with the new families hidden: **172 → 177 calls**, the two batches 4 of it (two colour, two moon-shadow) and the contact shadows 1; the merged `kit` mesh they vacate is deleted but was frustum-culled at that pose. NOT behind `ROOF_BLENDER` — that flag switches between two glbs of one object and there is nothing here to switch to** |

| the street's traffic runs on a stopwatch | position was `(phase * len + clock * v) % len`, so a car passed any point every `len / v` seconds for ever. The LAP is the unit of variation now — it falls out of the same arithmetic, so still closed form and still storing nothing — and each lap hashes a SKIP (30%) and a WAIT (half a slot). Car 0's gaps: flat 14.0 -> **14.3 / 14.0 / 27.6 / 14.3 / 27.7 / 13.6 / 28.0**. Both bounds are the SLOT, so nothing can converge: min same-lane separation over 1200 samples **0.66** (it was 0 for two cars drawing one slot). Then the SPEED: a pass now hashes its own speed off the lap too (`CAR_SPEED_JIT` 0.6, always at or above the lane base so it fits its window) — measured 12.23 / 11.84 / 17.62 / 15.25 / 16.88 against a base of 11.8. It rides on the PASS and not the car, since per-car speed on a shared line is the exact defect `Lane.v` was introduced to cure: a lane's cars are a CONVOY at one speed, one `len / k` apart, min same-lane separation measured at **85.6** = the gap exactly. Also BOTH ENDS OF A TRAFFIC LANE MUST LEAVE THE PLATE, or a car fades in pointed at the tower — endpoint distance to the tower's box **0 -> 134.2**, road lanes 46 -> 16. `CARS` 28 -> 80, mean drawn **29.8** vs a flat 28, same draw call. Also `CAR_LANE_MIN` 16 vs the walkers' 6 — a 6-cell lane is **1.2 s** of driving and never reaches full opacity; the plate's runs are 6, 33, 37, 49, 77, 84, 108, 156 cells, so it drops the two stubs and keeps all 44 | **landed** |

Emissive is `color x the sprite's own texels`, so `#f26a6a` read orange on a yellow face; `#ff2a2a` at peak 2.0 reads red. No new material, program or call | **landed. ## Standing notes

- **three is vendored** as `electron/three.global.js`, built from `tools/three-entry.js` via
  `node tools/build-three.mjs` (**no `make three` target** despite the comment). Core objects
  (`BatchedMesh`) are already bundled via `export * from 'three'`; `examples/jsm` ones
  (`GTAOPass`, `mergeGeometries`) need an explicit export **and** an entry in the `REQUIRED` guard.
- **`Occlusion` is the constraint on every batching idea.** Buildings must fade independently, so any
  merge must stay inside one building, or ship an ownership map. `pick()` size-guards city-wide
  meshes out (they land in `__occ.skipped()`), and a `userData.b` tag overrides the guard.
- **Batch only geometry that survives culling.** `BatchedMesh` frustum-tests every instance on the CPU
  each frame, so batching mostly-culled objects is a pessimization; an off-screen `InstancedMesh` is
  one bounding-sphere reject.
- **Per-call cost is not uniform.** Shadow draws are depth-only and averaged ~9.5µs against ~23µs for
  the survivors. Never convert a call count into a `submit` estimate.
- **An object that renders correctly only because of a material flag is a bug waiting for the next
  override pass.** `scene.overrideMaterial` replaces the material outright and drops `colorWrite`,
  `depthTest` and `polygonOffset`. Prefer real geometric separation, or `allowOverride: false`.
- **If an overlay is coplanar with a wall, give it a physical gap** (`OVERLAY_EPS`). `polygonOffset`
  alone has failed three times.
- **Anything that mutates an instance buffer after build must tell the `Occlusion` ghost** — the ghost
  is a snapshot taken at first fade, and it is the copy that draws while the real mesh is hidden.
- **A "turn it off" effect has to turn off every source.** A lamp has four: the pooled `SpotLight`, the
  additive `LightCone`, the fake ground shadow, and the glb's **emissive head** (independent of every
  light in the scene, and it feeds bloom directly).
- **`receiveShadow` is a uniform; `castShadow` is baked into the program key.** Flipping the first is
  free per-frame. Do not generalise from one to the other.
- `addRoofDetails` uses `Math.random()` for the type shuffle and yaw, so roof furniture is **not**
  seed-deterministic (unlike `Debris`) and reshuffles on re-entry. Unexamined; may be intentional.
- **ANYTHING THAT SITS ON A SURFACE WANTS A HALO UNDER IT, AND THAT IS THE DEFAULT — not a case to be
  argued each time.** A new prop, fitting, box or piece of kit gets `FacilityDetail.pool` on the
  surface it stands on, and `FacilityDetail.stand` on the wall behind it **when it is near one and is
  a large cuboid** — that second half is the qualifier, not the first. The whole family is a
  collector, one merged mesh and one material, so **the Nth halo is +2 triangles and nothing else**;
  the cost of a new emitter is one `stand`/`pool` call beside the `ContactMask.prop` the builder is
  already making. The interior fill is normal-blind by construction (`IndoorMask` is
  AmbientLight-shaped), so nothing else in the compound will ever ground an object for you — an
  ungrounded thing indoors is not a lighting bug that will get fixed later, it is a missing quad.
- **A HALO SAYS "THIS IS TOUCHING THAT", so the thing that decides whether one is emitted is the
  DISTANCE, and the distance is measured off the SLAB FACE and never off the cell.**
  `FacilityGeom.slab` insets a solid wall's room face a whole unit into the wall cell (a glazed one
  1.5), so anything built on its own cell — a bench — stands that far clear of the masonry and gets
  **no halo at all**: at 0.8 the quad reads as a stain on the wall, and moving the geometry to earn
  one was built and reverted. Anything built off the slab FACE — the fittings, the corridor casework,
  the lab kit — does touch, and takes one. If a new family stands against a wall, keep its back at
  **`FacilityStyle.STAND_GAP`**, which exists only to clear `HALO_EPS`: closer than that and the halo
  plane is INSIDE the object, cutting its side faces and drawing over them.

## Open leads (not yet acted on)

### Walls cost 3 calls per box — an atlas will NOT fix it, a texture array would

`flattenBox`'s floor is **one call per distinct texture**, so a building box costs 3 (walls, worn,
roof) and a parapet 2. In one follow-view census that was ~10 boxes × 3 = 30 calls; collapsing them
to 1 each saves ~20 of 158. **Unmeasured beyond that sample — get a real A/B before believing it.**

**Why the decal fix does not transfer.** Walls *tile*: `repeat.set(wWorld / TILE, …)`
(`Buildings.hx:72`) runs UVs 0→4 across a face and the wrap mode turns `u = 3.2` back into `0.2`,
which is what gives real brick at any building size without stretching. Wrapping is a property of the
**whole texture**, not of a sub-rectangle inside it — walk `u` past your cell's edge in an atlas and
you do not restart the brick, **you slide into the neighbouring image**. Decals could be atlassed
precisely because a decal quad samples its cell **once** (UV 0→1, nothing to wrap). Same word,
different problem. `fract()` in the shader fakes the wrap but breaks mipmapping — the UV jump at each
tile seam blows up the derivatives and the GPU picks the blurriest mip, a visible seam per tile
(fixable with `textureGrad` + padding, i.e. owning custom sampling for every wall in the game).

**The right tool is `DataArrayTexture`**: N full textures as layers, sampled
`texture(tMap, vec3(u, v, layer))`. Each layer is its own complete 0→1 space, so wrapping, mipmaps and
anisotropy all work per layer and brick in layer 3 cannot reach layer 4. `flattenBox` already bakes
per-face texture matrices into the geometry UVs — this adds a per-face `layer` attribute beside them
plus an `onBeforeCompile` patch to sample the array (same move as `instanceUV` in `DecalBatch`).

**The strategic half is bigger than the ~20 calls.** Every wall box would share **one material**,
which is the precondition for welding buildings into a few giant meshes — see the two-tier merge lead
below. The array does not remove that plan's real blocker (`Occlusion` fades one building at a time),
it removes the texture obstacle under it.

**Cost:** all layers must share size + format; the array is built by blitting every wall PNG into one
buffer (build step or load-time canvas); ~20 layers × 512² ≈ 20MB + mips; per-texture `roughness` has
to go uniform or ride another attribute; and every wall material becomes a custom shader we own.
Subsystem-scale. Weigh against a frame that currently runs **158 calls / 4.66ms submit / 11.4ms idle**
— i.e. ~30% used. **Measure on the weak-hardware target before spending this.**

### Two-tier static city merge — for the debug free-cam, not for players

**Read the scope line first:** this targets the **street-debug free-cam at a parallel angle**, the one
view that renders the whole city. The goal was making that dev view usable, not player framerate.

Gameplay does not need it, and that is measured, not assumed: the city is **226–233 buildings**
(`CityGen.generate`, seeds 1-3), while gameplay runs **158–288 calls** (follow 158, zoom-out ~202,
tactical 288). Fog + the far-plane clip + frustum culling already discard ~200 of ~230 buildings
before a draw is issued. **Do not cite this entry as a player-facing perf win.**

The idea: the city is static, so weld every piece sharing a texture into a few giant meshes — "all
brick walls citywide", "all coping", "all roofs" — ~10–20 meshes for the whole map. The free-cam then
draws ~200 calls instead of ~3,000. Standard merged-static-geometry technique.

**The blocker is `Occlusion`.** It fades an *individual* building while it blocks the camera→player
sightline, by owning that building's materials and easing their opacity. One welded mesh + one
material = all-or-nothing fading. The fix is tiering:

- **Near tier** — the handful of buildings that can actually occlude the player. Stays on today's
  individual fadeable meshes, code path unchanged.
- **Far tier** — everything else, welded, never fades (too far to occlude anyway).
- Re-tier on **area movement**, not per frame, re-welding when a building crosses the boundary.
  `Occlusion` only ever sees the near tier.

Notes: welding reuses the bake already written for `flattenBox`/`mergeBand` (per-face UV transform →
geometry UVs, world transform → verts) — the merge is the same vertex concat, and the new work is the
tiering + re-weld bookkeeping. Weld granularity is capped at one mesh per texture because each wall
texture is its own `Texture`; a `DataArrayTexture` (lead above) would lift that cap, though it does
**not** touch the `Occlusion` blocker. Transparent/cutout passes (windows are already `InstancedMesh`,
doors are alphaTest) can stay as-is. Watch the near/far boundary for pop — geometry is identical
either side (same bake), only fadeability changes.

**Verdict: deferred.** Only path to a big cut in the free-cam view, but it is a real subsystem —
tiering, `Occlusion` rework, re-weld on movement. Player-facing perf does not need it. Do it only if
the debug free-cam's usability is worth a subsystem.

### The floor — 32% of the frame, never attacked

Raw texture bandwidth, overdraw, the two RGBA16F composer targets and the `OutputPass` blit, measured
with all lighting and post off. Invariant across lamp counts (4.69 at pool 12, 4.55 at pool 0), which
is what makes it a real target rather than a measurement artefact. Suspects: stacked transparent
overlays (grime, wall decals, storefront bands and doors all draw *over* the wall via `polygonOffset`,
each a full lit fragment pass), and the FP16 composer target format. **No existing debug key isolates
overlay overdraw** — that instrumentation is the first step.
